# Item registry — closed

Work items with status `DONE` or `WONTFIX`. Open items live in
`item-registry-open.md`.

## Controlled status vocabulary (closed file)

| token | meaning |
|---|---|
| `DONE` | completed; delivering PRs are in the `PR` column |
| `WONTFIX` | decided not to implement; reason is in the description |

A `DONE` leaf may have zero or more PRs — operational work (e.g. config changes)
needs no PR; items delivered across several PRs may list them all. Umbrellas never
appear as `DONE` here directly — they are `DONE` only when all leaf children are
`DONE`/`WONTFIX`, and the generator emits them here once that condition holds.

Git history is the archive for per-PR detail, rationale, and iteration history.
This table holds the stable structured fact — what was decided and where to find it.

The `PR`, `deps`, `dependents`, and `refs/links` columns are each independent
first-class columns. The generated `.md` files are the source-of-truth views of
`registry/items.yaml` — do not edit them by hand (hand edits fail CI).

| <a id="i2"></a>**i2** | **On-SAM IDE memory model (edit buffer + IN/OUT paging; claim all free RAM, grow on demand)** — On-SAM IDE memory model — DECIDED (q36, Pete 2026-06-22). A SINGLE dynamic page pool (a resident page_owner[] allocator in section D, boot-sized from PRAMTP) shared by the editor AND the assembler: any subsystem alloc_page/free_page; a requester that can't get a page raises its own out-of-memory error. Supersedes the assembler's hardcoded off-axis pages 4-15 and FOLDS the i23 96KB-IN / i24 32KB-OUT ceilings into the pool. 256KB is FIRST-CLASS (~144KB doc ceiling; ~9 free pages); 512KB ~25 free pages. Pool exhaustion = refuse-with-message (the honest ceiling) — page persistence/SPILL is a DOWNSTREAM feature (depends on i2, never blocks it). Lazy edit/assemble spill: DOC blocks serialize to disk only when FREE can't satisfy an alloc, and only as many as needed (§4.5) — a 512KB machine touches disk zero times. IDE owns allocation for its session (rebuild BASIC ALLOCT only on exit). Spec: docs/specs/ide-memory-model-design.md. Implements the pool i41 §5.2 already assumes; lifts i23/i24 for free. | DONE | agent |  |  |  | docs/specs/ide-memory-model-design.md, i41, [i23](#i23), [i24](#i24) |

| <a id="i2b"></a>**i2b** | **Wire the page pool into the production assembler boot path: boot-time sizing + resident pool + paged boot self-test** — Second i2 brick (after i2a's standalone allocator). Makes the pp_* allocator LIVE in the production assembler: at boot, read PRAMTP to size the pool (pp_init marks [0,count) FREE, [count,32) absent), then RESERVE pages 0..15 — the base 256KB the current system occupies (0..3 BASIC/system/screen/DOS, 4 ENCTAB, 5..6 OUT, 7..12 IN, 13..14 payloads, 15 disasm), leaving expansion RAM (16..PRAMTP on a >256KB machine) as the FREE pool. The page_owner[] table is resident in section D (PP_TABLE_BASE). A boot self-test on the PAGED boot path (claim-all/free-all round-trip; reserved pages never move; spec §6) runs OFF-AXIS in the page-12 cluster (test_pagepool.asm) to stay out of the tight section-C test-variant budget; exercised by the harness TestBootSelfTestsPass + the SimCoupe matrix. The harness seeds PRAMTP=0x1F (emulation-first: model the ROM-set sysvar). ADDITIVE: does NOT migrate any buffer to the pool (the static pages 0..15 stay in use), so the self-test never writes pool-page contents — marking an expansion-RAM page FREE is safe here. The precise DOSFLG/VMPR survey (reserving the real DOS/screen pages, incl. any DOS in expansion RAM) and the NEW-BASIC reclaim are the CONSUMER bricks' responsibility (i23/i24 + the IDE shell), required before anything WRITES to a pool page. | <a id="i12b"></a>**i12b** | **Editor-testing input injection (SimCoupé -keyin end-to-end proof)** — DETERMINED (PR #502, Outcome B): SimCoupé's -keyin is a boot-prompt autotyper, NOT a general key feed for a running program; it cannot reliably deliver keystrokes to a post-boot SAM CODE program that polls FLAGS(&5C3B)/LASTK(&5C08) (the editor-input use case). Verified locally (SDL_VIDEODRIVER=offscreen, isolated HOME): a diagnostic stub booted with SIMCOUPE_KEYIN='BOOT\nABCABC\n', confirmed CanType() satisfied at the stub (read LMPR=&1F → Section A=ROM0, B=page0), and polled FLAGS for several seconds — bit5 never set and zero keys arrived. Root cause (SimCoupé Base/SAMIO.cpp + Base/Keyin.cpp): (1) the string loads ONCE at the WTFK startup screen with no re-arm; (2) keys stream only from the IMEXIT hook so the target needs interrupts ON; (3) Rst8Hook()'s `default: Keyin::Stop()` drops the pending string on any RST-8 code outside &00/&1d/&35/&13/&50, which DOS/BASIC trip during the boot→program handoff. The reliable, deterministic, CI-gated vehicle for editor INPUT tests is the Go harness InjectKeys (i138, keyboard_test.go) — the same LASTK/FLAGS mechanism driven directly; SimCoupé stays the boot/render gate via the existing fixture jobs. The full determination + reproduction lives in the -keyin comment block of tools/run-simcoupe.sh (single home). PR #502 also fixed a real run-simcoupe.sh bug: SimCoupé splits the parallel-printer capture across simc0000.txt, simc0001.txt… after inactivity gaps, and the wrapper globbed only the first file (silently dropping gapped output); it now concatenates all simc*.txt in numeric order. The core risk — one subagent's worktree filesystem changes leaking into another checkout — is already PREVENTED by three existing mechanisms, so no new hardening was required: (1) `.claude/worktrees/` is gitignored (commit 84d0224), so harness-native worktree files cannot pollute the main checkout's `git status` or be accidentally committed; (2) the Agent tool's `isolation: "worktree"` gives each mutating subagent its own git worktree, and CLAUDE.md discipline rules 3/5 forbid two writers in one checkout; (3) the rule-5 `git clean -fdx`-vs-nested-worktree hazard is latent only — no tool/Makefile/CI/`tools/` step runs `git clean -fdx`/`-ffdx` in the main checkout, and plain `git clean` skips worktree `.git` boundaries without `-ff`. The one observed residual is harmless: fully-orphaned `.claude/worktrees/agent-*` directories accumulate when the harness auto-clean doesn't fire (a stale Jun-12 leftover was found and removed); these are gitignored cruft (no `.git`, no admin entry, no checkout-leak) clearable any time via `git worktree prune` + `rm -rf .claude/worktrees/agent-*`. | <a id="i23"></a>**i23** | **Paged-IN HLOAD-ceiling lift (current ceiling: 96 KB = 6 pages 7..12, src/assembler.asm:49)** — Lift the IN-file 96KB ceiling by migrating the IN buffer to the i2 page pool. DESIGN (Pete 2026-06-23, option A): CONTIGUOUS-RUN, not non-contiguous scatter — SAMDOS HLOAD/HSAVE are contiguous-only (no seek/scatter; ldblk samdos c.s:575 + ctas c.s:318-374), so IN is allocated as one contiguous run via a new pp_alloc_run pool primitive. Reader hot-path inner loop (in_normalise_hl inc-advance + reader_next_kind copy) stays UNCHANGED; only the base page becomes a runtime var (IN_BASE_LMPR). No in_pages[] scatter. Pieces: (1) pp_alloc_run/pp_free_run (Go authority pagepool.go + Z80 pagepool.asm + harness test); (2) pool_boot frees IN pages 7..12 into the pool (reserve 0..6 + 13..15) + update i2b self-test; (3) dynamic base in reset_reader/reader_init/load_in_file; (4) load_in_file allocs the run (head-page alloc -> decode prefix size -> free -> alloc run_size -> HLOAD; fail_with_tag on no-fit, replacing the cp 7 bound). Ceiling = largest contiguous free run (256KB on 512KB SAM via 16..31; 96KB on 256KB unchanged, accepted under A). Verify: regression (base stays 7 for <=6 pages) + pp_alloc_run fragmented unit test + >96KB harness integration test (base lands at 16) + release-gate + SimCoupe. Plan: docs/plans/i23-in-pool-contiguous.md. True scatter loader deferred (Pete chose plain A). | DONE | agent | [#595](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/595) | [i2a](#i2a), [i2b](#i2b) |  |  |

| <a id="i25"></a>**i25** | **File-I/O error handler via DOSER (&5BC0)** — Surface SAMDOS file-I/O failures (HGTHD/HLOAD/HSAVE file-not-found, disk-full, wrong-type, ...) to the user instead of crashing the assembler. The app-facing error vector is DOSER (&5BC0), NOT (hksp) (see i185: (hksp) is zeroed on every hook entry — samdos/src/b.s:450-451 — and is SAMDOS-internal/NMI-only; an app handler there is never reached). Mechanism: port COMET's idiom (comet.asm prepare/dier/sproom, 1265-1382) — set (&5BC0) to a handler before file ops; ROM PTDOS does JP (DOSER) after every hook (rom DOSC :12977-12980,13003) with A = error number (0 on success). Scope: alt D (minimal, chosen) — the handler does AND A; RET Z on success, else converts the error into the existing diagnosed FAIL (jp fail with a load/save tag, reusing src/assembler.asm fail/fail_with_tag + src/print.asm); alt A (future enhancement) — per-error-number messages + stack recovery so the program could continue rather than halt. Also CORRECT the i183 harness, which models the wrong vector ((hksp)): model the DOSER post-hook dispatch (success + error) and treat an app-set (hksp) as zeroed, so the handler is verified against the real mechanism (emulation-first). Refs: docs/specs/samdos-file-io.md, comet.asm:1265-1382, src/loader.asm + src/main_loop.asm (hook call sites), tools/z80-test-harness-go/harness.go. | DONE | agent |  | [i183](#i183), [i185](#i185) |  |  |

| <a id="i27a"></a>**i27a** | **i27a: Go authority — Cortex-A53 835769 + 843419 errata detection/fix + --fix-cortex-a53-* flags + binutils cross-check** — Greenfield Go authority for BOTH errata as toggleable assemble/link-time fixes, off by default (Pete q37: on for A53/Pi3, off for later silicon). Faithful to binutils 2.44 bfd/elfnn-aarch64.c (research report in this PR). 835769 (NO page condition — that was Pete's conflation with 843419; it is a data-dep erratum): detect a memory op immediately followed by a 64-bit multiply-accumulate (mask aarch64_mlxl_p: (insn&0xff000000)==0x9b000000, op31 in {0,1,5}, Ra!=0x1f) with no RAW carve-out (aarch64_erratum_sequence); fix = insert NOP (0xd503201f) between them. Because we are the assembler (not a linker post-processing opaque objects), insert the NOP into the instruction stream and re-run layout so PC-relative refs recompute (binutils uses a branch-to-stub veneer ONLY because it cannot move placed code); NOP insertion cannot create new 835769 hazards so it converges in one re-pass. 843419 (intrinsically link/locate-time — needs final VA): detect ADRP at final page-offset 0xff8/0xffc (mask (insn&0x9f000000)==0x90000000) + the sequence predicate (mem-op, optional 1 non-branch insn, then LDST_UIMM (insn&0x3b000000)==0x39000000 whose Rn==ADRP Rd); fix = rewrite ADRP->ADR in place (no address shift) — for our ~21KB image the +/-1MB ADR range ALWAYS fits so stubs/veneers are never needed. Add --fix-cortex-a53-835769 and --fix-cortex-a53-843419 CLI flags (tools/sam-aarch64), thread post-Pass2 / post-layout. errata.go with the binutils predicates ported verbatim (cite file:line). Cross-check vs binutils-2.44 ld where a GNU toolchain is on PATH (principled skip otherwise). | <a id="i42"></a>**i42** | **CRC / signature in the .tbn file header (integrity check)** — WONTFIX (Pete 2026-07-01, q52). A .tbn integrity/CRC field is NOT warranted. (1) STORAGE already CRC-protects it: floppy FDC writes a hardware CRC-16 per sector (SAMDOS surfaces a CRC error as a read error, not silent garbage); Trinity SD blocks carry CRC + card ECC. (2) NETWORK: each hop is CRC-32-protected by the ENC28J60 Ethernet FCS (hardware TX-append + RX-discard); our UDP checksum is 0 (legal IPv4, per trinload build_udp_frame), so the ONLY uncovered vector is corruption INSIDE an intermediate switch -- ~never with ECC switches on a home LAN. (3) PRECEDENT: the closest analog, ELF (assembled binary + metadata), carries NO corruption checksum; a.out/COFF/base-Mach-O/user-mode-PE likewise delegate to storage/transport. Where executables DO add integrity (GNU build-id, code signing) it is for IDENTITY or ADVERSARIAL trust, not accidental corruption. (4) A CRC adds recompute-on-save complexity for a ~never risk. The q49 hard-fail-on-invalid-decode already turns gross structural corruption into an error, not a silent mis-assemble. IF EVER REOPENED (real corruption bites): use CRC-16 or the existing RFC-1071 one's-complement sum (ip_checksum), NEVER SHA-256 (adversarial tool, wrong + slow). | WONTFIX | agent |  |  |  | https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/blob/c0f62fa/docs/notes/m1-status.md |

| <a id="i70e"></a>**i70e** | **i70b shot finding: store_begin/bdos_find_free_record wedges on real B-DOS 1.5t (trinload-pushed) — diff vs the 1.5t authority + close the emulation gap** — RESOLVED via PR 838 (2026-07-03). ROOT CAUSE (authority-diffed vs bdos15t-beta6.annotated.dis, exactly as this item required): the http images built without NETBOOT_REAL_LISTREAD, so bdos_find_free_record issued rst 8 / defb 161 — a HARNESS-INVENTED hook. On real 1.5t, 161 = HLDBK (real handler, &46C5; dispatch table &839F admits 128-166), which falls into the SD/SPI driver: ALL 1.5t SD I/O runs under DI with unbounded &DC busy-polls and NO ESC-abort (tst.esc deleted 1.5a->1.5t), so the wrong-register call spins forever with interrupts off — hence the dead rx loop and power-cycle-only recovery. The Go oracle served 161 at its RST-8 seam, which is why emulation passed (the rule-7 gap). FIX (PR 838): (1) oracle panics on 161/162 + any unmodelled hook unless a test opts in — the wedge class reproduces in the harness (the pre-fix http dbg test tripped it at the exact rst 8 site); (2) http images build with NETBOOT_REAL_LISTREAD (raw CMD17 — the sd_push/i319a hardware-proven mechanism, per this item and q27/trinity-record-detection-design); (3) the bdos_seam scan cluster requires an explicit list-path flag — forgetting it is now a BUILD error. HSAVE mirrors used-records into the SPI card model so the CMD17 scan sees them (multi-file provisioning re-proven). Follow-up: i354 (delete the gated synthetic machinery). The i70b re-shoot is unblocked once this merges. | DONE | agent | [#838](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/838) |  | [i70b](#i70b) |  |

| <a id="i120"></a>**i120** | **Windowed TFTP (RFC 7440) for the netboot client/server — throughput optimization** — i118 drops windowsize from the client RRQ to stay a correct lock-step (one ACK per block) client. That costs round-trips on large transfers (an 800 KB .mgt at blksize 1428 ~574 lock-step round-trips over the SAM's slow ~20-80 KB/s SPI stack). Windowed delivery (window 4 -> ~4x fewer round-trips) is the throughput win. Requires the receiver to ACK only the last block of each window (+ handle lost-block/out-of-window rewind) on both client (i82) and server (i83/i95); re-add windowsize to ClientOptionSet only once windowed logic exists on both sides + is host-verified. | DONE | agent |  |  |  | tools/netboot-oracle/tftp/, src/netboot/netboot_client.asm, docs/notes/tftp-protocol-research.md, i118, [i82](#i82), [i83](#i83), i95 |

Rationale: the only benefit was SD-card SPACE — but a ~32GB card holds ~40,000 disk images at 819200 B each, while the entire published SAM catalogue is ~1,000 disks (<1GB). Space will never be the constraint, so the dedup machinery (content-addressed naming, name->hash manifest, streaming chunk-compare, copy-on-write on divergence, dedup-reference bookkeeping against the shared-storage confirm gate) is pure complexity for zero real benefit. | <a id="i137"></a>**i137** | **build-disk netboot ceiling check doesn't distinguish section class (i125 follow-up)** — WONTFIX (2026-06-24): the premise is a stale paging model. Original concern: build-disk -netboot rejects only >&10000 (32768B), but a 'section-C bootable must fit &8000-&BFFF (<=16384) or it loads into ROM1 at boot and crashes'. That is FALSE: at boot LMPR=&1F has ROM1 OFF (bit 6 clear), so section D (&C000-&FFFF) is RAM, not ROM1 (docs/notes/sam-paging.md L119-123, citing ROM disasm L13066 'LD A,1FH;OUT(250),A'; ROM1 only appears with LMPR=&5F). LOAD CODE 32768 deposits >&BFFF bytes straight into section-D RAM and runs there — proven in SimCoupe by 'make secd-loadability' and modelled in netboot_boot_test.go; serve/client/http deliberately use the 32768 budget (i145b section-D CSD overlay) and i96 serve is hardware-confirmed. So there is no ROM1-crash gap: build-disk's &10000 hard ceiling is correct/complete, and the 16384-vs-32768 split is a per-image self-imposed DESIGN budget whose single home is the make fit-check (tools/netboot-boot-fit-check.sh). Adding a -netboot-section-c flag would reject valid section-D images and duplicate that policy (single-source-of-truth). The item also miscategorised serve as section-C-only (it uses section D). | WONTFIX | agent | [#646](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/646) |  |  | tools/build-disk/main.go, tools/netboot-boot-fit-check.sh, [i125](#i125), i119 |

| <a id="i183"></a>**i183** | **Harness: model the SAMDOS file-not-found / HSAVE error longjmp (currently a silent no-op)** — Emulation-first prerequisite for i25. The Go harness (tools/z80-test-harness-go/harness.go HGTHD/HLOAD/HSAVE cases) models an unregistered HGTHD file as a SILENT NO-OP (currentFile=nil), so a real SAMDOS file-not-found error manifests downstream as a cryptic 'jumped into garbage' trap rather than the actual derr -> (hksp) longjmp. | <a id="i185"></a>**i185** | **Correct SAMDOS file-I/O error references: app vector is DOSER (&5BC0), not (hksp)** — i25/i183 and the reference docs (docs/specs/samdos-file-io.md, docs/notes/sam-stub-audit.md) framed graceful file-I/O error handling around installing an (hksp) handler. That is wrong: the SAMDOS hook dispatcher zeros (hksp) on EVERY hook entry before the hook body runs (samdos/src/b.s:450-451), so an app-set (hksp) is never seen by derr (d.s:430-437) -- (hksp) is SAMDOS-internal (NMI snapshot only, d.s:606/745). The app-facing error vector is DOSER (&5BC0), a BASIC sysvar (always mapped, no paging dance) that ROM PTDOS dispatches via JP (DOSER) after every DOS hook (rom DOSC, docs/sam/sam-coupe_rom-v3.0_annotated-disassembly.txt:12977-12980,13003); COMET uses exactly this (comet.asm:1265-1382). samdos-file-io.md:174 already notes &5BC0 is COMET's error vector -- internally inconsistent with its (hksp) caveat. Scope of THIS item: fix the reference docs. | <a id="i213"></a>**i213** | **Trinity EEPROM capability signature + version (interfacing code auto-detects our patch, never assumes it)** — Trinity EEPROM/firmware version + identity stamp: when we customise the Trinity EEPROM (i135c — stripes restore, boot-a-record-on-startup), write a small magic signature + version byte at a stable offset, plus a read helper. JUSTIFICATION (Pete, 2026-06-23, refined): NOT for SD-capability negotiation (record access through the EEPROM B-DOS is transparent, so no capability check is needed). The real need: the B-DOS actually in control is NOT guaranteed to be the one we flashed — B-DOS can be loaded from FLOPPY (the traditional DOS-load path), which is the normal case for anyone WITHOUT our customised ROM chip (not universal). So our software can't assume the EEPROM's patched firmware is present; the stamp lets it DETECT which firmware/patch is actually running and handle a mismatch gracefully. SCOPE: just the stamp + read helper (not the heavyweight per-capability auto-detect tied to the WONTFIX i214). The write rides i135c; the stamp-format + read helper are owner:agent. Relatedly this is WHY i214 (fix BD_RECORDS in the EEPROM) was dropped — a floppy-loaded B-DOS bypasses any EEPROM fix, so our direct-SD record code (i141/i145, frozen-interface, version-independent) is the right layer, and it is already faithful-16-bit which is ample. | DONE | agent | [#701](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/701) |  |  |  |

That is wrong: a HALT is terminal only when interrupts are disabled (di;halt, the boot-end convention); an EI;HALT (IFF1==true) is waiting for the 50 Hz frame interrupt and must resume, as real hardware does. | <a id="i270"></a>**i270** | **Hardware finding: serve TFTP WRQ (write) handshake hangs on silicon while RRQ (read) works — root-cause the never-tested SD-write/HSAVE path** — HARDWARE FINDING (2026-06-26, real SAM): serve TFTP WRQ (write) hangs on silicon while RRQ (read) works. tcpdump: RRQ for a missing name gets an ephemeral-port not-found reply (~10ms); WRQ gets ZERO reply, SAM stays ARP-reachable -> it RECEIVES the WRQ but hangs before the first handshake reply. SETTLED root cause (i292 cleanup; memory feedback_trinity_sd_write_settled_truths): NOT flaky B-DOS hooks (that was a false conclusion defended across sessions) and NOT a need to reimplement Colin's write. Two real causes were found and are settled: (1) the A=drive-select bug (our serve left main A = sector number, so the first .mgt sector selected the floppy -> un-timed FDC poll = hang) — fixed by A=2 for HWSAD/HRSAD, hardware-confirmed (i280, settled truth A9; trinity-sd-z80-interface.md §8a); (2) our own per-block full ENC ereset disturbing the shared-microcontroller SD state between writes (A3) — removed by the B-DOS-direct fix i293 (zero re-arm). The lasting discipline from this finding (CLAUDE.md rule 8): diff Trinity-HW code against Colin's B-DOS / the Trinity docs FIRST, never against the Go emulator. Open children: i270b/i270c keep the Go SD-write model honest vs the authority. The genuine remaining hardware open question is the in-context HRECORD rst-8 crash (D1, under i293). | DONE | agent |  |  |  |  |

RULE: for ANY Trinity integration (EEPROM, SD card, ENC28J60 Ethernet) the AUTHORITY is the working Z80/Colin SAM code (Colin B-DOS 1.5t SD/EEPROM driver; the proven ENC driver) — the Go model is DERIVED FROM and VERIFIED AGAINST it, never the reverse. A Go-model comment claiming to BE the authority for HW behaviour is the anti-pattern. Codify as a CLAUDE.md rule + ideally a provenance check/lint flagging Trinity-HW code that cites the Go model as authority instead of the SAM/Colin source. First concrete instance = i270's fix. | <a id="i287"></a>**i287** | **Model the Trinity shared-read-latch interleave rule in the ENC emulator + diagnostic** — Pete's rule (manual:IMG_20260617_162617, DISCOVERY_REPORT §7): the three data ports &DD/&DE/&DF alias ONE microcontroller read-back latch; after a peripheral MUX switch you must clock the newly-selected peripheral before reading the latch, else IN returns the PREVIOUS peripheral's stale byte ('do not interleave OUTs to different peripherals before reading back'). Encoded in tools/netboot-oracle/z80/enc28j60.go: track which peripheral last wrote the latch (lastClockedBy); a data IN that returns a foreign peripheral's byte is a violation (StrictInterleave panics; InterleaveViolations() records count+context). Controls in trinity_interleave_test.go (positive: deliberate ENC->SD cross-read FIRES; negative: clock-then-read is clean). The WRQ-push setup asserts zero violations across every push test. DIAGNOSTIC RESULT: our own-CMD24 serve does NOT violate the rule (zero violations even in the wire-level interleaved pushes) — RULES OUT an interleave violation as the cj.mgt-push bug. Confirmed structurally: the Trinity &DC-&DF ports are accessed ONLY from main-line drivers, never from an interrupt handler (trinload installs no custom ISR; the ROM frame ISR is KEYSCAN/clock, doesn't touch Trinity), so improper interleaving cannot occur. The remaining hardware issue is the per-block self-heal SLOWNESS, not a protocol-rule violation. Most other documented rules (BUSY-before-OUT drop, read-after-BUSY, &DC status-exempt, PUSH/POP, auto-null, SD block/byte addressing) were already modeled. | DONE | agent | [#753](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/753) |  |  |  |

| <a id="i294"></a>**i294** | **Determine the real .mgt->record sector ordering on hardware (track-major vs side-major)** — RESOLVED: a Trinity SD record stores a SAM disk SIDE-MAJOR, a .mgt stores it TRACK-MAJOR. record LBA = side*800 + 10*track + (sector-1) (B-DOS 1.5t conv.de @ &A151, the hardware read authority); .mgt offset = ((cyl*2+side)*10 + sec-1)*512 (cylinder-interleaved, samfile). CONFIRMED 3 independent ways: conv.de traced; samdisk 3.x record.cpp write loop (for head{for cyl} = side-major); LIVE samdisk 3.8.8 empirically (write a .mgt to a file-backed HDF record -> 1600/1600 side-major permutation; read-back round-trips to source bar the +232 stamp). The reorder is a CONTAINER-layer transform (independent of the disk's DOS): UNCONDITIONAL + DOS-agnostic (a BDOS-stamped .mgt still reorders 1600/1600 + survives repeated round-trips). This is why sd_push's track-major push produced non-bootable records (directory aligns at LBA 0, file data on side1/higher tracks scrambled -> '108 End of file'). The FIX is tracked as i315 (sd_push SAM-side reorder). Final on-hardware boot-proof rides i315's validation. Plan: docs/plans/sd-push-sector-reorder.md. | DONE | agent | [#784](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/784) |  |  |  |

| <a id="i307"></a>**i307** | **B-DOS 1.5t 16-bit records clamp is data-safe at ALL card sizes (analysis, answered)** — Pete asked: can B-DOS 1.5t itself write a record body/seek over the record LIST for some card size (16-bit overflow)? ANSWER (deterministic): NO, safe at all sizes. The &A452 clamp caps records1 at 65536 (base <= 2050); BD_RECORDS is 16-bit (last.record <= 65535); the &A113 seek multiply is genuine 32-bit; so max seek base+1600*(N-1) <= ~104.9M << 2^32 and always > base -- never wraps below base -- and the list region (65568-entry capacity) always over-covers <= 65535 records. Only cost: wasted capacity on >51GB cards, never corruption. Regression guard: TestSeekMult16x32IsGenuine32Bit. Mark DONE via #773 (analysis + guard shipped). | DONE | agent | [#773](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/773) |  |  |  |

| <a id="i314"></a>**i314** | **Reconcile the firmware-fetch record naming: Z80 storage_sink_leaf names records by hex(name[0:3]) but the Go authority bdos.SpanRecordName is content-addressed hex(hash[0:3])** — Discovered during i231b-b4i (#782 review). RULE-6 divergence in the hardware-gated HTTP firmware-fetch store path. The Go authority tools/netboot-oracle/bdos/span.go:52 SpanRecordName(blobHash [32]byte, index) returns a CONTENT-ADDRESSED name: fmt.Sprintf('%02x%02x%02x%03d', blobHash[0], blobHash[1], blobHash[2], index) — hex of the first 3 bytes of the blob's SHA-256 HASH + the zero-padded record index. But the Z80 src/netboot/http_main.asm storage_sink_leaf passes FW_STORE_NAME_PTR (the manifest NAME ptr set by store_begin), not a hash, to fw_span.asm fw_span_record_name — which hex-encodes the first 3 bytes of whatever HL points to. So the Z80 names records hex(NAME[0:3])+index, NOT hex(HASH[0:3])+index. fw_span.asm's own comment ('Mirrors bdos.SpanRecordName(blobHash...)', 'Emit hash[0], hash[1], hash[2]') says it should take a HASH — inconsistent with the caller passing the name. This is PRE-EXISTING (not introduced by #782; the bootable was byte-identical) and hardware-gated (the http_main store path is unverified on real hardware, CLAUDE.md §5), and #782's new tests accurately document the CURRENT name-based behavior. RECONCILE: (a) is the Z80 a port bug — storage_sink_leaf should pass a hash pointer (CONN_PINNED_HASH or the computed digest) to be content-addressed like the authority — OR (b) are the authority + fw_span.asm comment stale and name-based naming is the intended design? Check the serve side (netboot_serve reads 'the same convention' per http_main.asm) — does it expect name-hex or hash-hex? If the persist + serve sides agree (name-hex) the round-trip works but diverges from the authority; if they disagree, records can't be found. Per rule 6 the Go authority is the encoding truth unless there's a documented reason to diverge (like the plain-vs-suffixed SpanPlan choice). May escalate to a qN if the design intent needs Pete. Relates to i101 (serve record naming), the fw_span/http_main store leaf. | DONE | agent | [#850](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/850) |  |  |  |
| <a id="i315"></a>**i315** | **sd_push: reorder .mgt sectors track-major -> side-major on write so pushed records boot (SAM-side)** — RESOLVED FINDING (i294): a Trinity SD record stores a SAM disk SIDE-MAJOR. B-DOS 1.5t conv.de (&A151, the hardware read authority, rule 8) maps (track,side,sector) -> record LBA = side*800 + 10*track + (sector-1): side 0 = LBA 0..799, side 1 = LBA 800..1599. A .mgt container is TRACK-MAJOR (cylinder-interleaved: offset(cyl,side,sec) = ((cyl*2+side)*10 + sec-1)*512, per samfile). sd-push.py streams .mgt sectors sequentially (track-major) to record LBAs, so a pushed record shows its directory (track0/side0 aligns under both orders) but FAILS TO BOOT ('108 End of file, 0:1') — side-1/higher-track file data is scrambled. CONFIRMED 3 independent ways: (1) conv.de traced; (2) samdisk 3.x record.cpp write loop (for head { for cyl } = side-major); (3) LIVE samdisk 3.8.8 empirically on Pete's Mac — writing a .mgt to a file-backed HDF record yields a 1600/1600 side-major permutation, samdisk read-back round-trips to source (only the +232 stamp differs). The reorder is UNCONDITIONAL, DOS-AGNOSTIC, container-level: a BDOS@232-stamped .mgt STILL reorders 1600/1600 and survives repeated round-trips (Pete's +232-gate hypothesis empirically FALSE) — the .mgt container is always track-major regardless of the DOS inside. The +232 'BDOS' 4-byte stamp is a SEPARATE optional signature (samdisk --fix, at record-body sector0 +232; a working record like Comet lacks it; not required to boot). FIX (SAM-side per Pete — the SAM owns the .mgt->record translation so any pusher just streams track-major .mgt): in sd_push's receive/write path, map each incoming .mgt sector index m (0..1599) to its side-major record linear before bd_record_write_hw: side=(m%20)//10; cyl=m//20; sec1=m%10; record_linear = side*800 + cyl*10 + sec1. Do NOT patch the user's .mgt host-side; do NOT bake the reorder into the generic bd_record_write_hw (other callers). VALIDATE: (a) emulation via the netboot harness (assert the written record body is side-major); (b) HARDWARE (Pete-gated shared-card write, family of i295): push a side-major record + boot it on the SAM -> CJ's Elephant. Plan: docs/plans/sd-push-sector-reorder.md. | DONE | agent | [#785](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/785) |  | [i319](#i319) |  |

| <a id="i323"></a>**i323** | **Measure + assess sd_push network throughput on hardware (the ~10-13 KB/s question)** — RESOLVED (2026-07-02, differential hardware measurement: a no-SD-write sd_push build isolating the network leg + an in-binary meter build counting PIC busy-wait spins and card-busy polls in the finalize reply; local throwaway builds, emulation-smoked first, nothing merged). MEASURED on the real SAM + 64GB SDHC: full path 74.2 ms/sector = 6.9 KB/s (steady 69.9-75.0; consistent with the i319a 112 s/1600). ATTRIBUTION: (1) network leg 25.6 ms/sector, WINDOW-INDEPENDENT (w1 25.71 vs w4 25.64) so host python pacing + ack turnaround = 0 - the earlier ~32 ms RX estimate was high; (2) SD-write leg 48.5 ms/sector = Z80 manual-mode bit-bang (~195 T/byte sdc_out sandwich x 522 B = 17.4 ms nominal) + SD PIC busy-wait (MEASURED 638 spins/sector = 14-21 ms) + CARD BUSY ZERO (mtr_busy=0 over 1000+ writes); the ~1.55x nominal-vs-measured gap = SAM memory contention. CONCLUSION: bit-bang-bound (Z80 per-byte loops + PIC relay), NOT card-bound, NOT network/host-bound. SAM-side headroom -> i338 (auto-null OUTI data phase + drop the double copy, est. ~50 ms/sector); beyond that the PIC relay is the floor. Full numbers: trinity-capabilities.md 'Measured on hardware'. | <a id="i339"></a>**i339** | **sd_push mid-stream degradation cascade: one CMD24 hiccup triggers full-init-ladder-per-block (~15 s/sector) + near-deaf ENC; fix the fail->reinit amplifier + raise the 256-poll busy budget** — OBSERVED LIVE (i323 campaign, 2026-07-02, real SAM + 64GB SDHC): a full-push meter run degraded at sector ~759 from ~74 ms/sector to ~15 s/sector for the rest of the stream. Meters: mtr_wait=8,318,715 SD PIC busy-wait spins (~180 s) vs mtr_busy=0 (the CARD was never busy) - so the collapse is the SD-path state machine, not the card. Mechanism (fits all evidence): one CMD24 failed its data-response check (blwr_reject skips the busy loop, matching mtr_busy=0) -> sdc_card_ready=0 -> EVERY subsequent write runs the full sdc_init_ladder incl. the &38 SD-wake whose PIC settling is seconds (the i242 finding) and leaves ENC RX near-deaf (acks ~1 per 15 s) -> if writes keep failing under the settling PIC, the cascade self-sustains. Aftermath: a SUBSEQUENT sd_push run failed its SD startup entirely (never answered discovery; parked on a fail-hold); trinload itself stayed healthy (7 ms discovery, pure-ENC); only a power cycle recovered the SD path. Data safety HELD (record list verified intact before/after; only the claimed record written). This is very likely the i278/i296 wedge class seen live, and plausibly what Pete's original slow-push (~10-13 KB/s, 'well over a minute') sampled. FIX SHAPE: (a) on a mid-stream CMD24 failure, retry the CMD24 without the &38 wake ladder (re-select + re-issue; bound retries) instead of clearing sdc_card_ready into a per-block full re-init; (b) raise the 256-poll (~14-19 ms) blwr_busy budget toward the SD-spec 250 ms write-busy allowance (a legitimately slow write would trigger the same cascade; tonight's card was never busy, but the budget bug is latent); (c) emulation-reproduce the cascade first (model a one-shot data-response glitch + the &38 settle) per CLAUDE.md rule 7. Evidence + method: trinity-capabilities.md 'Measured on hardware' + the i323 closing description. | DONE | agent | [#815](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/815) |  |  |  |

| <a id="i363"></a>**i363** | **http store leaf names records by hex(filename) not content-hash: storage_sink_leaf passes FW_STORE_NAME_PTR to fw_span_record_name (which expects the blob SHA-256 digest ptr)** — The i70b shot-4 hardware read-back (i362 read-record of REC 199) revealed the HTTP store leaf names each HSAVE'd record 'hex(filename[0..2])+NNN' instead of the intended content-addressed '<hash6><NNN>'. ROOT CAUSE: http_main.asm:628-630 storage_sink_leaf does 'ld hl,(FW_STORE_NAME_PTR); call fw_span_record_name' — but fw_span_record_name's contract (fw_span.asm:82) is HL = ptr to the 32-byte blob SHA-256 DIGEST (it hex-encodes hash[0..2]). FW_STORE_NAME_PTR is the logical FILENAME ptr. So for LICENCE.broadcom the record was named '4c4943000' (hex of 'LIC'=4c 49 43, +index 000) instead of the correct 'c7283f000' (hex of the pinned SHA-256 c7283ff5..., +000). Note FW_SPAN_HASH (fw_span.asm:46) is declared 'in: 32-byte digest' but NEVER written by any code — the intended hash buffer is dead. FIX (CLAUDE.md rule 6, port bdos.SpanRecordName which uses blobHash): thread the current file's 32-byte SHA-256 digest ptr — the manifest entry's sha256 field in FW_MANIFEST (fw_source.asm) — from prov_start/store_begin through to storage_sink_leaf and pass THAT to fw_span_record_name (or populate FW_SPAN_HASH from it). Content-addressed retrieval (TFTP serve-by-name / the picker) needs the right name, so provisioned files are otherwise unretrievable. TEST GAP that let it through: fw_span_test.go asserts the function in isolation with a correct hash ptr; the http_main faithful store test does NOT assert the stored record NAME. Add an integration assertion (faithful store leaf -> read back the record's directory entry name == bdos.SpanRecordName(pinnedHash,0)) so it can't regress. Hardware-confirmed via i362. | DONE | agent | [#850](https://github.com/petemoore/sam-aarch64/pull/850) |  | [i70b](#i70b) |  |
